Crude sewer walls give way to precisely carved ancient geometric patterns. The architecture shifts from municipal to sacred. Air visible in torchlight. | Party | Deepening dread | Mid-wide, advancing |\n   194→| 56 | Trap Corridor | Corridor lined with trap mechanisms: pressure plates, wall holes at chest height, suspicious ceiling groove. Party moves single-file, testing each step. | Party | Careful tension | Mid-shot, corridor |\n   195→| 57 | Door Rush | Jorgen holds open a heavy stone door as the party rushes through one by one, squeezing past a pressure plate mechanism. Urgency. The door is closing. | Jorgen, party | Urgent escape | Mid-action, doorway |\n   196→| 58 | Descending Ceiling | VERTICALLY COMPRESSED PANEL. Massive stone ceiling descending in a large underground room. Claustrophobic pressure. Figures crouch, pressed down. The aspect ratio itself creates the feeling. | Party | Claustrophobia, panic | Wide, compressed vertical |\n   197→| 59 | KNEEL | POV straight up at the descending ceiling, terrifyingly close. Carved into the ancient stone in dried blood: the word KNEEL. Close enough to touch. The stone demands submission. | None (ceiling POV) | Dread, submission | Extreme upward POV |\n   198→| 60 | Spiral Staircase | A spiral staircase descending into darkness, viewed from above. {"parentUuid":"640d1ac4-aad9-42a0-b350-a98d12eca861","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"00d91d86-ad69-404a-aad8-2a048e6b309c","agentId":"a47eb08dc424f0771","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JHDxtLvNtJiPCnWnHfvPVC","type":"tool_result","content":"| 71 | The Sealed Door | A massive ancient STEEL DOOR on the far wall. Sealed with concentric rings of forged metal. At its center: the CREST OF THE RAVEN QUEEN -- stylized raven, wings spread, talons gripping ambiguity. | None (door focus) | Mythic barrier | Wide, door dominant |\n| 72 | Door Close-Up | Extreme close-up of the sealed door surface. Concentric metal rings inscribed with writing in a language predating common tongue. The craftsmanship is inhuman in its precision. | None (detail) | Ancient authority | Extreme close-up |\n| 73 | Party Reacts to Door | The party before the sealed door, weapons lowered. Four-colored backlight silhouettes and illuminates faces. Each face shows a different reaction: determination, fear, fascination, fury. | Full party | Divided awe | Mid-wide, backlit |\n| 74 | The Question | Wide establishing of full ritual chamber. Waterfalls frame composition: red and green left, orange and blue right. CENTER: the sealed door with the Raven Queen's crest. Party small before it, dwarfed. | Full party, chamber | The unanswered question | Wide establishing |\n| 75 | Walking Away | Wide shot: party walks toward camera, away from sealed door. Four supernatural waterfalls blaze behind them. Four-colored shadows stretch forward impossibly long. Posture: we will return. | Full party | Unfinished purpose | Wide, dramatic shadows |\n\n---\n\n### Act 7: Credits and Tease (Panels 76-80)\n\n**Setting**: Abstract/graphic design space.\n**Tone**: Mythic identity, ensemble promise, divine foreboding, anticipation.\n**Color Temperature**: Warm gold typography against dark atmospheric backgrounds; each waterfall color isolated in sequence; final pure black with gold text.\n\nThe credits sequence serves triple duty: establishing the series identity, reminding the audience of the ensemble, and embedding the four-waterfall color system as the visual signature of the series.\n\n**Panel Breakdown**:\n\n| Panel | Title | Description | Characters | Mood | Camera |\n|-------|-------|-------------|------------|------|--------|\n| 76 | Title Card | HEROES OF MARDOV in ornate gothic fantasy typography -- gold-edged, warm-toned, raven-feather motifs in the letterforms. Over a dark atmospheric background: the ritual chamber in deep shadow. | None (typography) | Series identity | Centered graphic |\n| 77 | Party Silhouettes | Nine character silhouettes in ensemble composition. Each distinctive: dragonborn's massive frame, gnome's tiny stature, tiefling's horns, druid's wolf-edge posture. Against four-colored waterfall light. | All nine (silhouettes) | Ensemble promise | Wide graphic, backlit |\n| 78 | Raven Queen Crest | Heraldic crest on pure black: the Raven Queen -- stylized raven with wings fully spread, every feather forged in detail, talons gripping an ambiguous object. The symbol that seals the door. | None (heraldry) | Divine authority | Centered graphic, black |\n| 79a | Waterfall -- Blood Red | Horizontal band: blood-red supernatural waterfall. Thick, hot, luminous. Steaming on stone. The color of sacrifice. Dense, viscous, interior light. | None (element) | Sacrifice | Horizontal band |\n| 79b | Waterfall -- Acid Green | Horizontal band: acid-green supernatural waterfall. Pulsing organic corruption. Bubbling, alive with biological motion. The color of pestilence. | None (element) | Pestilence | Horizontal band |\n| 79c | Waterfall -- Mercury Orange | Horizontal band: mercury-orange supernatural waterfall. Metallic fluid refracting light like molten iron. The color of war. Brilliant, aggressive, sharp. | None (element) | War | Horizontal band |\n| 79d | Waterfall -- Soul Blue | Horizontal band: soul-blue supernatural waterfall. Translucent, shimmering, barely liquid. Half-formed faces within. The color of death. | None (element) | Death | Horizontal band |\n| 80 | Final Black Tease | Pure black frame. Center: warm gold gothic text matching title treatment: \"Next time: The ritual. The sacrifice. And the crystal hearts.\" Faintest glow -- the sealed door's outline, almost invisible. | None (text) | Anticipation | Black with gold text |\n\n---\n\n## Character Guide\n\n### Player Characters (The Party)\n\n**Sprocket** -- Gnome Diviner\n- *Appearance*: ~3.5 ft tall. Warm tan skin. Golden-yellow spiked hair. Large dark analytical eyes. Gap-toothed grin with a sharp edge. Bushy sideburns. Brass goggles (usually on forehead).\n- *Clothing*: Ornate academic robes, ink-stained. Pockets full of lenses and instruments.\n- *Personality*: Feverishly intellectual. Sees the world as a system of patterns to decode. His calculation occasionally blindsides him with wonder -- the ritual chamber overwhelms his analytical framework.\n- *Role in Pilot*: The eyes of the party. His reaction shots anchor the audience's understanding of supernatural events.\n- *Panels*: 21, 22, 65, 69\n\n**Brigit** -- Human Druid (Shapeshifter)\n- *Appearance*: Young woman. Dark auburn hair tangled with leaves and twigs. Pale porcelain skin with freckles. Green eyes -- scanning, alert, predatory.\n- *Clothing*: Practical earth tones. Barefoot. Looks like the forest did not fully let her go.\n- *Personality*: Quiet, watchful, more animal than she lets on. She does not assess things as a person does; she reads ecosystems. Joins the recruitment because something in the land is wrong and she cannot walk away.\n- *Wolf Form*: Grey-brown, lean, alert. The shift between forms is liquid and organic.\n- *Panels*: 23, 24, 25, 26\n\n**Stalwart** -- Dragonborn Paladin\n- *Appearance*: Seven feet tall. Muscular warrior build. Golden-yellow reptilian scales. Spiky golden mane. Dark amber eyes. Sharp dragon-like facial features.\n- *Clothing*: Heavy dark metallic armor with a scarlet surcoat. Greatsword on back.\n- *Personality*: Commands every room by existing. The weight of duty and lineage is visible in how he carries himself. When he speaks Draconic to the kobolds, it is not a trick -- it is heritage asserting itself.\n- *Role in Pilot*: The physical anchor. His size and authority provide the party's first real victory (the kobold standoff).\n- *Panels*: 27, 28, 53, 54\n\n**Nyx** -- Tiefling Warlock\n- *Appearance*: Petite build. Pale pinkish-lavender skin. Small curved horns. White/pale blonde voluminous curly hair.\n- *Clothing*: Cobalt-blue cloak. Leather journal always close.\n- *Personality*: Private, intense, writing constantly. Her journal is her anchor. She leaves her inn room with the posture of someone leaving something behind -- her past, her safety, or both.\n- *Panels*: 29, 30\n\n**The Wild Mage** (unnamed in pilot) -- Human Mage\n- *Appearance*: Young woman. Wild-eyed. Ill-fitting, mismatched mage robes.\n- *Signature*: Uncontrolled white-gold magic sparking involuntarily from fingertips. Equal parts terror and exhilaration.\n- *Panels*: 31a\n\n**Tiny Rick** -- Human (Short Stature)\n- *Appearance*: 4 feet tall, adult human proportions (NOT a halfling). Angular, wrong for his size. Daggers at belt.\n- *Personality*: Aggressively compensating. Elbows through crowds. Eyes of a cornered predator who chose to attack first.\n- *Panels*: 31b, 42\n\n**Dallor** -- Gnome Rogue\n- *Appearance*: Small stature. Quick clever hands. Darting calculating eyes. Practical traveling clothes with many pockets.\n- *Personality*: Quiet, opportunistic, always slightly richer than when he arrived. Shares grudging mutual respect with Tiny Rick.\n- *Panels*: 31c, 42\n\n### NPCs\n\n**Finkle** -- Gnome Bureaucrat/Drill Sergeant\n- *Appearance*: Tiny gnome in an immaculate tailored suit that still does not fit. Flanked by two enormous half-ogre bodyguards.\n- *Personality*: Pure disdain. Absolute authority. Stands on crates to achieve something approaching normal height. His triangle management diagram (FINKLE at top, EVERYONE ELSE at bottom) tells you everything.\n- *Role in Pilot*: Comedy anchor and authority figure for the recruitment sequence.\n- *Panels*: 35, 36, 37, 38\n\n**Jorgen** -- Human Ranger (Betrayer)\n- *Appearance*: Handsome, professional. Gold military tunic (torn and bloodied on arrival). Scratched face, composed bearing.\n- *Personality*: Presents as a competent, wounded military professional. His mask is convincing. When it drops in the ritual chamber, the shift is total -- cold, purposeful, calculating. He led the party underground deliberately.\n- *Role in Pilot*: The guide who becomes the threat. His betrayal in the ritual chamber is the pilot's dramatic pivot.\n- *Panels*: 43, 48, 57, 62, 67, 68\n\n**The Smith** -- Unnamed Blacksmith\n- *Appearance*: Muscular, anonymous. Defined by his work, not his identity.\n- *Role in Pilot*: The hands that forge the god-killer weapon. His forge sequence introduces supernatural elements before the party encounters them.\n- *Panels*: 16, 17, 19\n\n---\n\n## Visual Style Notes\n\n*Drawn from the pacing study and production design.*\n\n### Overall Aesthetic\n- **Gothic anime motion webtoon** -- not clean cel-shaded modern webtoon. Every surface has history: worn stone, scarred wood, weathered cloth.\n- **Motivated light sources only**: torches, moonlight, candlelight, forge-glow, supernatural waterfall light. No generic illumination.\n- **Vagabond-influenced backgrounds**: rough brush stroke energy, lived-in detail. The world existed before this story and will continue after.\n- **Berserk's violence weight**: injuries matter. The tavern fight leaves marks. Jorgen's scratches are not decorative -- they establish his cover story.\n\n### Color System\nThe pilot establishes the **four-waterfall color grammar** that will define the entire series:\n\n| Waterfall | Color | Concept | Hex Range |\n|-----------|-------|---------|-----------|\n| First | Blood-red | Sacrifice | Deep crimson, hot, luminous |\n| Second | Acid-green | Pestilence | Toxic, pulsing, organic |\n| Third | Mercury-orange | War | Metallic, brilliant, aggressive |\n| Fourth | Soul-blue | Death | Translucent, cold, spectral |\n\nThese four colors should appear ONLY in the ritual chamber and credits in the pilot. Their absence from the rest of the episode makes their appearance overwhelming. In future episodes, traces of these colors will bleed into the surface world as the sealed door's influence spreads.\n\n### Camera Language\n- **Establishing shots**: Wide, slightly elevated. Used for location introductions (abbey, town, chamber).\n- **Character introductions**: Mid-shot or close-mid, intimate. Each character gets framing appropriate to their nature (Stalwart: low angle; Sprocket: interior/cozy; Brigit: forest/liminal).\n- **Action sequences**: Alternate extreme close-up (impact/expression) with wide shots (consequence/geography).\n- **The ritual chamber reveal**: Must use a FULL-PAGE panel. The scroll distance to traverse it should feel like entering the space.\n- **Breath panels**: After the tavern fight and after the betrayal -- near-empty panels that let the audience land.\n\n### Panel Density Rhythm\nFollowing the pacing study's recommendation:\n- Acts 1-2: Lower panel density. Wide panels. Let the world breathe.\n- Act 3 (Forge): Tighter panels for the crafting sequence, one wide dramatic for the completion.\n- Act 4 (Characters): Two-panel vignettes per character, wider for the yard, tighter for the tavern.\n- Act 5 (Descent): Increasing density and compression as the space gets smaller. The KNEEL panel should feel physically oppressive.\n- Act 6 (Chamber): The full-page reveal breaks all preceding density expectations. Then rapid-fire panels for the betrayal.\n- Act 7 (Credits): Return to wide, graphic, breathing space.\n\n---\n\n## Music and Sound Cues\n\n*Suggested audio beats for the motion webtoon format. Sound design should be sparse -- silence is a tool.*\n\n| Panel(s) | Audio | Notes |\n|----------|-------|-------|\n| 1 | Ocean wind. Distant ravens. Stone creaking. | No music. The abbey's age is in its sounds. |\n| 2-5 | Low ambient drone. Candle crackle. Parchment rustle. | War room atmosphere only. |\n| 6 | Silence. Map being unfolded. | The map is the sound. |\n| 7 | Ocean waves, distant. Single window light hum. | Night ambience. Loneliest moment. |\n| 8 | Dawn birdsong cut by hoofbeats. Minimal score begins -- low strings. | Music enters for the first time. Sparse, traveling. |\n| 9-10 | Hoofbeats on stone. Leaves rustling. At panel 10: horses stopping. Wood creaking. | The claw marks need no sound -- the silence around them is worse. |\n| 11-14 | Town bustle fading in. Hammering, voices, gulls. | Environmental sound replaces score. |\n| 15-19 | FORGE SEQUENCE: Hammer strikes as rhythm. Hissing quench. Blue-gold energy hum. Building to panel 19: a sustained resonant tone as the god-killer is raised. | The forge has its own soundtrack. Panel 19 is the first crescendo. |\n| 20 | Town dusk ambience. Score recedes to nothing. | Breath moment. |\n| 21-32 | Each character vignette gets its own sound signature. Sprocket: pen scratching, page turning. Brigit: forest sounds, then bare feet on stone. Stalwart: dock wood, heavy boots. Nyx: candleflame, quill on paper. Quick cuts: magic sparking, crowd noise, market chatter. | Sound design IS characterization here. |\n| 33-40 | Finkle's entrance: door SLAM. His voice: high, precise, cutting. Background: recruits shuffling, wooden weapons clacking. | Comedy timing in the audio. |\n| 41-44 | Tavern warmth (murmur, ale, laughter) shattered by door SLAM (Jorgen) then immediate COMBAT: glass breaking, kobold shrieks, blade sounds. | The tonal shift from warmth to violence should be instantaneous. |\n| 45-47 | Post-combat: dripping ale, settling debris, heavy breathing. Then quiet. The note is read in near-silence. | Breath sequence. The audience needs this too. |\n| 48-50 | Descent begins. Footsteps on wet stone. Echoes. Water dripping. Each sound amplified by underground acoustics. | Sound design becomes increasingly claustrophobic. |\n| 51-54 | Kobold encounter: hundreds of small sounds -- clicking, breathing, claws on stone. Stalwart's voice in Draconic: deep, resonant, DIFFERENT from human speech. Then: silence as kobolds retreat. | The Draconic speech should be the most striking vocal moment in the pilot. |\n| 55-59 | Trap corridor: stone mechanisms shifting. Doors grinding. Then the CEILING: deep stone-on-stone grinding. Dust falling. The sound of immense weight moving slowly. | Panel 59 (KNEEL): ALL SOUND STOPS except the character's breathing. |\n| 60-61 | Spiral staircase: footsteps echoing at different depths. At bottom: a low THRUM -- the waterfalls felt before seen. | The ritual chamber's sound precedes its image. |\n| 62-64 | Jorgen's face panels: heartbeat. Chamber entrance: the thrum builds. PANEL 64 (full-page reveal): FULL WATERFALL SOUND. Four distinct liquid sounds, each with its own pitch and texture. Overwhelming, layered, alive. | This is the pilot's sonic climax. The waterfalls are four voices. |\n| 65-68 | Sprocket's reaction: waterfall sound muted to emphasize his breathing. Jorgen's betrayal: blade drawn -- metallic ring cuts through waterfall ambient. | The betrayal sound should cut the waterfall wall. |\n| 69-70 | Combat in the chamber: metal, shouting, waterfall sound constant beneath. Post-combat: waterfalls unchanged, steady, indifferent. | The waterfalls do not react to human violence. This is the point. |\n| 71-75 | The sealed door: a deep resonant hum from the metal itself. As the party approaches, the hum shifts pitch. Walking away: the hum fades slowly, reluctantly. | The door has a voice. It is waiting. |\n| 76-80 | Credits: A composed theme begins -- the series theme. Gothic, orchestral, with four-note motifs corresponding to the four waterfall colors. Final panel: theme cuts to silence. Text appears with a single low tone. | The theme should establish the series identity. The silence after it is the hook. |\n\n---\n\n## Panel-by-Panel Storyboard Reference\n\n*Complete index of all 80 panels (including sub-panels) with production metadata.*\n\n| # | Filename | Act | panel-80-credits-final-black-tease.png | 7 | YES | None | Black frame, gold text tease |\n\n---\n\n## Production Status\n\n**Panels Rendered**: 49 of 80 (including sub-panels) -- 61% 8GB)\n**Estimated Render Time for Remaining**: ~15-20 minutes (at ~30s/image, 31 images)\n\n### Rendering Priority (Suggested Order for Remaining Panels)\n1. **Panel 64** quality pass -- the ritual chamber full page is the pilot's centerpiece\n2. Panels 62-68 (ritual chamber sequence) -- the climax must be visually consistent\n3. Panels 2-7 (abbey interior sequence) -- opening tone-setter\n4. Panels 8-14 (journey and town) -- world-building foundation\n5. Remaining character panels (25, 26, 34, 36, 39, 40, 41, 44)\n6. Descent gaps (47, 49, 52, 55, 56, 60, 61)\n7. Credits (72, 73, 75, 77)\n\n---\n\n## Thematic Through-Lines\n\n### The Sealed Door\nThe central mystery of the pilot. A death goddess sealed something beneath this town, and the seal is ancient, deliberate, and inscribed in a language that predates human civilization. The door is not a barrier to treasure -- it is a warning. The pilot's question is not \"what's inside?\" but \"why does the Raven Queen need something sealed?\"\n\n### The Four Waterfalls\nSacrifice (red), Pestilence (green), War (orange), Death (blue). These four concepts are the pillars of whatever ritual this chamber was built to contain. Their colors will become the visual DNA of the entire series. In the pilot, they appear ONLY in the underground chamber and the credits -- their power comes from their absence everywhere else.\n\n### Strangers Becoming a Party\nThe pilot traces the oldest D&D story: strangers meet, are thrown together by circumstance, and discover they need each other. Finkle's recruitment yard is the mechanical assembly. The tavern is the first fragile social bond. The dungeon is where the bond is tested. The betrayal is where it hardens into something real.\n\n### Jorgen's Betrayal\nThe ranger who rescues the party is the one who endangered them. This inverts the \"quest-giver NPC\" trope and teaches the audience that in Mardov, trust is earned, not granted by narrative role. Jorgen's mask is good enough to fool the party and the audience. When it drops, the pilot retroactively becomes a different story.\n\n---\n\n*Document version 1.0 -- Mardov Pilot A Story Bible*\n*For all creative production. This is the definitive reference for what happens, when, and why.*\n*Next time: The ritual. The sacrifice. And the crystal hearts.*","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"ecbb6e78-f724-4de7-a4df-f262d2085a1f","timestamp":"2026-03-18T03:09:05.441Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"640d1ac4-aad9-42a0-b350-a98d12eca861","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"a5125120-9cbe-408a-953a-457fed0eba08","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"fluffy-cuddling-tome"}

Establishes tone: ancient weight, institutional weariness.\n- Key panels: Abbey on cliff (panel 1, rendered), sealed scroll arrival (panel 3), war room (panel 2), night decision (panel 7)\n\n**Act 2: The Journey** (Panels 8–14)\n- Riders depart, landscape travel, merchant cart discovery, port town arrival and preparation.\n- Key panels: Riders departure (8, rendered), claw-marked cart (10), town gate (12), notice board (14)\n\n**Act 3: The Forge** (Panels 15–20)\n- Supernatural sword forging sequence.
